*Completion of the proof of Theorem <a href="#dyn:conditional" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="dyn:conditional">43</a>.* Take a subsequence of the stationary projected path laws with a weak limit. By Lemma <a href="#dyn:tightness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="dyn:tightness">47</a>, diagonal extraction on the horizons $1,2,\ldots$ gives a consistent continuous-path law on $[0,\infty)$, denoted by $Q$. Restrictions are consistent because the limits are continuous at the restriction times.

<a id="dyn:laplace"></a>
Let $f_0,\ldots,f_k\in C(S^2)$ and $\alpha_1,\ldots,\alpha_k>0$. Integrate the stationary finite-dimensional expectation at times $0,t_1,t_1+t_2,\ldots,t_1+\cdots+t_k$ against $\prod_{j=1}^k e^{-\alpha_jt_j}\,\mathrm dt_j$. The Markov property and the resolvent identity give exactly $$\label{dyn:laplace}
 \mu_n\!\left[
  (f_0\circ\pi_n)R^n_{\alpha_1}
  \left((f_1\circ\pi_n)R^n_{\alpha_2}
    \left(\cdots(f_{k-1}\circ\pi_n)
                R^n_{\alpha_k}(f_k\circ\pi_n)\cdots\right)\right)
 \right].$$ Successive applications of Lemma <a href="#dyn:resolvent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="dyn:resolvent">45</a>, including its varying-data assertion, and weak convergence of the measures show that <a href="#dyn:laplace" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="dyn:laplace">[dyn:laplace]</a> converges to $$\mu\!\left[f_0R_{\alpha_1}
       \left(f_1R_{\alpha_2}
          \left(\cdots f_{k-1}R_{\alpha_k}f_k\cdots\right)\right)
       \right].$$ By the semigroup-resolvent relation for the closed form, this is the same integrated expectation for the stationary diffusion $B$.

It is also the integrated expectation for $Q$. On bounded time boxes this follows from weak path convergence, since evaluation at fixed times is continuous at continuous paths. Dominated convergence then integrates over the gaps. The contribution of the complement of a large time box is bounded uniformly by the tails of the product of exponentials, times $\prod_j\|f_j\|_\infty$.

The product expectations under $Q$ and under $B$ are bounded continuous functions of $(t_1,\ldots,t_k)$, including zero gaps. Uniqueness of multivariate Laplace transforms makes these functions equal: one may apply one-dimensional uniqueness successively in each coordinate, and then use continuity to remove almost-everywhere qualifications. Product tests determine probability measures on the compact product $(S^2)^{k+1}$. Thus $Q$ and the law of $B$ have the same finite-dimensional distributions, and hence the same law on continuous paths. Every subsequential limit is the same, proving the theorem. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-38"></a>
#### The clock and the metric-space conclusion

We now return to the random maps. The exact relation between the graph energy and the prescribed walk fixes the clock without any subsequence-dependent factor.

<a id="stmt-dyn-energy-clock"></a>
<div id="dyn:energy-clock" class="lemma">

**Lemma 48** (Exact energy of the accelerated walk). *For the half-edge walk of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, $$-\langle u,nL_nu\rangle_{L^2(\mu_n)}=\frac12\mathsf e_n(u).$$ Equivalently, $nL_n=-\tfrac12\mu_n^{-1}H_n$, where $\mu_n^{-1}$ denotes division by vertex mass.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-dyn-energy-clock"><summary>Proof of stmt-dyn-energy-clock</summary>

*Proof.* The generator convention gives $H_n=-\deg\,L_n$. Since $\mu_n(v)=\deg(v)/(2n)$, $$-\sum_v\mu_n(v)u(v)nL_nu(v)
   =\frac12\sum_vu(v)H_nu(v)
   =\frac12\sum_{\{v,w\}}(u(v)-u(w))^2.$$ Loops contribute zero to both sides. Their degree contributions are already included in the first identity, so no holding-time correction is needed. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-dyn-energy-clock-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-dyn-energy-clock</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.* <span id="dyn:main-proof" label="dyn:main-proof"></span> Start with any subsequence. Retain the random variable $A_n$ of Proposition <a href="#gr:green-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gr:green-bound">38</a> together with all the contour, spatial, and local and whole-sphere array variables used in Theorem <a href="#sph:energy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sph:energy">36</a> and Proposition <a href="#sph:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sph:comparison">37</a>. Its uniform first-moment bound gives tightness, so these variables admit a joint further extraction. Apply the sphere results to this enlarged extraction and use a Skorokhod representation. On the resulting probability-one event, their conclusions hold and $A_n$ converges to a finite random variable. In particular $\sup_n A_n<\infty$ after discarding finitely many initial terms.

Fix a realization in this probability-one event. Its limiting quantum sphere is now a fixed measured conformal sphere. The projections $\pi_n$, measures, edge mesh, uniform energy bounds and recovery, and harmonic compactness satisfy hypotheses (i)–(iii) of Theorem <a href="#dyn:conditional" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="dyn:conditional">43</a>. The realized bounded $A_n$ gives hypothesis (iv). The Liouville form is exactly <a href="#dyn:continuum-form" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="dyn:continuum-form">[dyn:continuum-form]</a> with $\mu=\mu_h$. By Lemma <a href="#dyn:energy-clock" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="dyn:energy-clock">48</a>, the chain in that deterministic theorem is $Y^n_t=X^n_{nt}$. Consequently its conditional projected path law converges to the conditional law of $B^h$, for every fixed finite horizon. All probabilities in this invocation are taken on the individual fixed graphs. The random object being compared is the conditional probability law itself.

It remains to express this comparison in the metric $\delta_T$. The round topology and the $D_h$ topology agree on the compact limiting sphere, so the identity map between them is uniformly continuous in both directions. The path-law convergence just proved therefore also holds in $D([0,T],(S^2,D_h))$.

The correspondences in Proposition <a href="#sph:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sph:comparison">37</a> have distortions tending to zero. The standard correspondence gluing constructs a common metric space containing isometric copies of all $(V_n,a_nd_n)$ and $(S^2,D_h)$, with $$\sup_{v\in V_n}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_Z\bigl(v,\pi_n(v)\bigr)\longrightarrow0.$$ For completeness, for each $n$ attach its copy to the sphere by cross-distances obtained by taking the infimum over corresponding pairs, adding numbers $\varepsilon_n\downarrow0$ larger than half the respective distortions; use the shortest-path metric on the union, identifying only the common sphere, and then take the completion. This preserves the metrics of every copy. The images become close in Hausdorff distance, and their measures become close in Prokhorov distance.

Apply the embedding to each coordinate of a chain path. Its distance in the common Skorokhod space from its $\pi_n$-projected path is bounded by the displayed supremum, using the identity time change. The corresponding probability laws therefore have Prokhorov distance tending to zero. Combining this with the projected law convergence shows that all three terms defining $\delta_T$ tend to zero in the coupled extraction.

We have obtained almost-sure convergence in a further coupling from every original subsequence, to the prescribed quantum sphere and its conditional diffusion law. The subsequence principle gives convergence in distribution through all positive integers $n$. Forgetting the root, the spanning tree, and the contour marks leaves precisely the states in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. The same choice $c=1$ works for every time horizon. ◻

</details>

      "caption": "A triple contact in height coordinates. The shaded strips indicate positive intervals of shared quantum-boundary heights; they are not drawings of Euclidean seams.       "pdf_page": 12,
      "source_evidence": [

          "anchor": "inp:triple-figure",
          "exact_quote": "A triple contact in height coordinates.  The shaded strips\nindicate positive intervals of shared quantum-boundary heights;\nthey are not drawings of Euclidean seams.  The outer pair is linked\nbelow $H$, while above $H$ each outer run is link",
          "source_object": "fig:0001"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[x=0.85cm,y=0.9cm,>=Stealth,\n                    every node/.style={font=\\small}]\n \\fill[blue!7] (-0.15,2.1) rectangle (6.15,2.55);\n \\fill[blue!7] (-0.15,0.4) rectangle (6.15,0.85);\n \\draw[->,gray] (-1.7,0) -- (-1.7,3.1) node[above] {height};\n \\draw[thick] (0,0.15) -- (0,2.95) node[above] {$b$};\n \\draw[thick] (3,1.5) -- (3,2.95) node[above] {$w$};\n \\draw[thick] (6,0.15) -- (6,2.95) node[above] {$d$};\n \\draw[->,blue!65!black,thick] (0.08,2.3) -- (2.92,2.3);\n \\draw[->,blue!65!black,thick] (3.08,2.3) -- (5.92,2.3);\n \\draw[->,blue!65!black,thick] (0.08,0.62) -- (5.92,0.62);\n \\node[above] at (1.5,2.3) {$b\\to w$};\n \\node[above] at (4.5,2.3) {$w\\to d$};\n \\node[below] at (3,0.62) {$b\\to d$};\n \\draw[densely dashed] (-1.9,1.5) -- (6.4,1.5);\n \\node[left] at (-1.9,1.5) {$H$};\n \\fill (3,1.5) circle (2pt);\n \\node[anchor=west,fill=white,inner sep=2pt] at (3.25,1.73)\n            {$s_w^a+m_w^a$};\n \\draw[red!65!black,very thick] (-0.65,0.45) -- (-0.65,2.5);\n \\draw[red!65!black] (-0.85,0.45) -- (-0.45,0.45);\n \\draw[red!65!black] (-0.85,2.5) -- (-0.45,2.5);\n \\node[anchor=east,rotate=90,red!65!black] at (-0.93,2.45)\n            {uncapped band};\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "A schematic in height coordinates. A vertical axis on the left is labeled \"height\", with a horizontal dashed line at level $H$. Three vertical lines are labeled $b$, $w$, and $d$ from left to right. A vertical shaded reddish strip to the left of line $b$ is labeled vertically as \"uncapped band\". Three horizontal directed arrows show connections between runs: an arrow $b \\to w$ above $H$, an arrow $w \\to d$ above $H$, and a blue arrow $b \\to d$ below level $H$. On line $w$ at height $H$, a marked level is labeled $s_w^a + m_w^a$."
    },

(-.25,.05)\n   .. controls (.55,-.15) and (1.6,.27) .. (2.7,-.03);\n \\draw[orange!80!black,thick]\n   (.08,-2) .. controls (-.26,-1.2) and (.23,-.6) .. (.02,.05)\n   .. controls (-.18,.7) and (.23,1.25) .. (-.02,2);\n \\draw[blue!75!black,thick] (-2.15,-.42)--(-2.15,.42);\n \\draw[blue!75!black,thick] (2.15,-.42)--(2.15,.42);\n \\draw[orange!80!black,thick] (-.42,-1.5)--(.42,-1.5);\n \\draw[orange!80!black,thick] (-.42,1.5)--(.42,1.5);\n \\node[blue!75!black,anchor=south] at (1.7,.49) {primal crossing};\n \\node[orange!80!black,anchor=west] at (.53,1.35) {dual crossing};\n \\fill (0,0) circle (1.3pt);\n \\node[anchor=north west] at (.1,-.14) {$z$};\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "Two narrow intersecting tubes shown in projection. A horizontal wavy blue band is labeled \"primal crossing\" with an arrow pointing to it.     {
      "caption": "The two annular comparisons. Rotating the condenser current gives a dual potential with period $C_{m}$ and hence $λ ≤ 1$. An exact unit-period dual recovery pairs with the unit condenser jump to give $λ ≥ 1$. This is a schematic of the test annulus; the projected discrete graph need not resemble a regular mesh.",
      "figure_id": "en:fig-reciprocity",
      "pdf_page": 35,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "en:fig-reciprocity",
          "exact_quote": "The two annular comparisons.  Rotating the condenser current\ngives a dual potential with period $C_m$ and hence $\\lambda\\le1$.\nAn exact unit-period dual recovery pairs with the unit condenser jump\nto give $\\lambda\\ge1$.  This is a schematic",
          "source_object": "fig:0003"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[scale=1.05,>=Stealth]\n \\fill[gray!10,even odd rule] (0,0) circle (1.45) (0,0) circle (.55);\n \\draw (0,0) circle (1.45);\n \\draw (0,0) circle (.55);\n \\node[font=\\small] at (0,0) {$u_m=0$};\n \\node[anchor=east] at (-1.52,0) {$u_m=1$};\n \\foreach \\a in {40,140,220,320}{\n   \\draw[blue!65!black,->,thick]\n       (\\a:.64) -- (\\a:1.29);\n }\n \\draw[red!65!black,->,thick] (18:1.04)\n       arc[start angle=18,end angle=153,radius=1.04];\n \\node[red!65!black,anchor=south] at (0,1.54)\n       {dual period $C_m$};\n \\node[blue!65!black,anchor=north] at (0,-1.6)\n       {primal current $\\dd u_m$};\n \\draw[->,thick] (1.9,.6) -- (3.1,.6);\n \\node[align=center] at (2.5,.05) {unit-period\\\\recovery};\n \\begin{scope}[xshift=5cm]\n  \\fill[gray!10,even odd rule] (0,0) circle (1.45) (0,0) circle (.55);\n  \\draw (0,0) circle (1.45);\n  \\draw (0,0) circle (.55);\n  \\draw[red!65!black,->,thick] (18:1.04)\n       arc[start angle=18,end angle=330,radius=1.04];\n  \\node at (0,0) {$\\alpha_m$};\n  \\node[red!65!black,anchor=south] at (0,1.54)\n       {exact period $1$};\n  \\node[anchor=north] at (0,-1.6)\n       {$|\\langle\\dd u_m,\\alpha_m\\rangle|=1$};\n \\end{scope}\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "Schematic diagram showing two annular comparison setups connected by a rightward horizontal arrow labeled \"unit-period recovery\". On the left annulus, the outer boundary has label $u_m = 1$, the inner disk is labeled $u_m = 0$, a red counterclockwise curved arrow above is labeled \"dual period $C_m$\", and a blue counterclockwise curved arrow below is labeled \"primal current $du_m$\". On the right annulus, the inner disk is labeled $\\alpha_m$, a red counterclockwise curved arrow above is labeled \"exact period 1\", and the label below is \"$|\\langle du_m, \\alpha_m \\rangle| = 1$\"."
    }
